Profiling update consolidation across the retention and generalization factors from Eq. 11 , both lacking a scenario-fixed ceiling.
The bottom row identifies the binding bottleneck for each scenario, showing where later cycles erode gains and where updates stay within sampled goals. MR MG GW EM ① Retention factor S ζ S_{\zeta} : does the cycle- k k gain survive subsequent updates? ✜Dominant substrate Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} , except training-free methods Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} , or ℳ \mathcal{M} , 𝒯 \mathcal{T} , ℐ \mathcal{I} if training-free Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} , or ℳ \mathcal{M} , 𝒯 \mathcal{T} if training-free Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} , or ℳ \mathcal{M} , 𝒯 \mathcal{T} , ℐ \mathcal{I} ✜Iteration robustness under f ζ f_{\zeta} untested beyond a few cycles compounding hacking within single runs [ 9 , 129 ] sustained by trajectory replay [ 14 , 17 ] , no forgetting on held-out environments [ 15 ] fixed-loop continual RL only [ 95 , 161 ] ✜Dominant failure mode long-cycle drift compounding reward hacking distribution shift forgetting and distribution shift ② Generalization factor G ζ G_{\zeta} : does the gain transfer beyond 𝒢 k \mathcal{G}_{k} ? ✜Transfer locus parameters (any goal) parameters, or memory and skills (on use) parameters, or memory and skills (on use) parameters, or skills and harnesses (on use) ✜Experience coverage in 𝒟 k \mathcal{D}_{k} tasks synthesized over unlabeled media a fixed prompt pool the evaluation task set or stream [ 162 , 163 , 104 , 164 ] one simulator suite ✜Achieved generalization scope cross-task [ 7 , 5 , 22 ] in-distribution, partly cross-benchmark [ 129 , 165 ] in-distribution, one platform in-distribution, rare sim-to-real [ 166 , 18 ] Primary bottleneck of 𝒬 k UC \mathcal{Q}_{k}^{\mathrm{UC}} Retention Retention Generalization Generalization Localization Constraint. Even with high F ν F_{\nu} , a trajectory set is directionally unproductive if L ν L_{\nu} covers a vanishing fraction of environment actions, and FA accordingly remains viable only while 𝔼 τ ​ [ L ν ​ ( τ ) ] \mathbb{E}_{\tau}[L_{\nu}(\tau)] suffices for the grain f ζ f_{\zeta} requires. Temporal granularity fixes the scoring unit along τ \tau : the terminal outcome for episode-level scores, the action u t u_{t} for step-level scores, and the output y t y_{t} decoded into u t u_{t} for token-level scores.
Because L ν L_{\nu} counts environment actions, a single-action episode has L ν = 1 L_{\nu}\!=\!1 , and credit within a long reasoning chain y t y_{t} lies outside L ν L_{\nu} even where process reward models assign it [ 153 ] .
Episode-level feedback suffices when the task reduces to one discrete outcome, step-level process supervision becomes necessary as horizons lengthen and terminal success grows too sparse [ 153 , 167 , 168 , 18 , 169 ] , and token-level credit remains rare outside independently checkable reasoning chains [ 170 ] . Credit-assignment cost determines how much of that grain a cycle can afford, step-level scores requiring a process model or per-step estimates whose cost grows with horizon length [ 153 ] .
Granularity is thus an FA choice that reaches f ζ f_{\zeta} only through its signal. Asymmetric Vulnerability. The product in Eq. 10 admits only conditional, one-directional compensation between its two factors.
Higher F ν F_{\nu} from cross-model verification or understanding-generation duality offsets moderate localization loss only when an unbiased anchor exists [ 10 , 128 ] , whereas finer L ν L_{\nu} yields no directional gain once F ν F_{\nu} is gameable [ 9 , 159 ] .
Equal ceilings F ¯ \bar{F} therefore do not imply identical bottlenecks.
Multimodal reasoning and GUI/Web navigation both admit a high F ¯ \bar{F} ( Table IV ), through program-computed answers [ 64 ] and per-task state scripts in sandboxed environments [ 115 , 17 ] , yet reasoning fixes L ν = 1 L_{\nu}\!=\!1 and is bounded by the realized F ν ( k ) F_{\nu}^{(k)} of its consensus and self-judged signals [ 5 , 160 , 171 ] , while navigation scores whole long-horizon episodes and is bounded by L ν L_{\nu} [ 16 , 135 , 144 ] .
Multimodal generation instead faces a low F ¯ \bar{F} on open-ended prompts, where self-rewards and perception-only instruments are readily gamed [ 9 , 10 ] , and embodied manipulation loses both factors on hardware [ 172 , 173 , 19 , 145 ] .
Fidelity-bounded signals thus misdirect the loop and localization-bounded ones under-constrain it, making F ¯ \bar{F} a positioning axis and L ν L_{\nu} a within-regime variable. III-D Stage 3: Update Consolidation (UC) UC writes the update that f ζ f_{\zeta} computes from 𝒟 k \mathcal{D}_{k} and V ν ​ ( 𝒟 k ) V_{\nu}(\mathcal{D}_{k}) into one or more persistent components of 𝒜 k = ( Φ θ k , ℳ k , 𝒯 k , ℐ k ) \mathcal{A}_{k}\!=\!(\Phi_{\theta_{k}},\mathcal{M}_{k},\mathcal{T}_{k},\mathcal{I}_{k}) , as 𝒞 3 \mathcal{C}_{3} requires.
Writes to the episode-scoped buffer ℳ short \mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{short}} through Ψ \Psi fall outside UC, and V ν V_{\nu} -based selection of what to write remains in FA.
UC must write updates that persist undegraded across subsequent cycles and generalize beyond the cycle- k k goals, a constraint on retention and one on generalization.
We summarize this bottleneck through an effective-consolidation decomposition, 𝒬 k UC := S ζ ( 𝒜 k : k + j + 1 ) ⋅ G ζ ( 𝒜 k + 1 ; μ 𝒢 ) , \mathcal{Q}_{k}^{\mathrm{UC}}\;:=\;S_{\zeta}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{k:k+j+1}\bigr)\cdot G_{\zeta}\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{k+1};\,\mu_{\mathcal{G}}\bigr), (11) where Δ k ​ ( g ) = J ⁡ ( 𝒜 k + 1 , g ) − J ⁡ ( 𝒜 k , g ) \Delta_{k}(g)\!=\!J(\mathcal{A}_{k+1};g)\!-\!J(\mathcal{A}_{k};g) is the cycle- k k gain on goal g g , 𝒢 k \mathcal{G}_{k} is the support of 𝒈 k \boldsymbol{g}_{k} , and μ 𝒢 \mu_{\mathcal{G}} is the fixed evaluation distribution of Eq. 6 .
Here, S ζ ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] S_{\zeta}\!\in\![0,1] is the fraction of the cycle- k k gain on 𝒢 k \mathcal{G}_{k} that survives j j further cycles, min ⁡ { 1 , max ⁡ { 0 , 𝔼 g ∼ μ 𝒢 | 𝒢 k ​ [ J ⁡ ( 𝒜 k + 1 + j , g ) − J ⁡ ( 𝒜 k , g ) ] / 𝔼 g ∼ μ 𝒢 | 𝒢 k ​ [ Δ k ​ ( g ) ] } } \min\{1,\,\max\{0,\,\mathbb{E}_{g\sim\mu_{\mathcal{G}}|\mathcal{G}_{k}}[J(\mathcal{A}_{k+1+j};g)-J(\mathcal{A}_{k};g)]\,/\,\mathbb{E}_{g\sim\mu_{\mathcal{G}}|\mathcal{G}_{k}}[\Delta_{k}(g)]\}\} for a positive denominator, and G ζ ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] G_{\zeta}\!\in\![0,1] is the μ 𝒢 \mu_{\mathcal{G}} -measure of goals outside 𝒢 k \mathcal{G}_{k} with Δ k ​ ( g ) > 0 \Delta_{k}(g)\!>\!0 , renormalized to the complement of 𝒢 k \mathcal{G}_{k} .
The gain and the two factors correspond to the adaptivity, retention, and generalization goals proposed for self-evolving agents [ 31 ] , and S ζ S_{\zeta} normalizes by the cycle- k k gain the change on earlier goals that backward transfer records as a signed difference [ 94 ] .
Like F ν F_{\nu} , both factors are defined through J J and hence on R R , leaving 𝒬 k UC \mathcal{Q}_{k}^{\mathrm{UC}} diagnostic as well, and it exposes two failure modes. As S ζ S_{\zeta} falls toward zero, later cycles erase or distort the cycle- k k gain. As G ζ G_{\zeta} falls toward zero, the update stays confined to 𝒢 k \mathcal{G}_{k} .
Concretely, S ζ S_{\zeta} is bounded by the substrate that f ζ f_{\zeta} writes and its iteration robustness , while G ζ G_{\zeta} is governed by the transfer locus of that substrate and the experience coverage of 𝒟 k \mathcal{D}_{k} ( Table V ). Retention Constraint. Substrate and iteration robustness act on S ζ S_{\zeta} at different levels: the substrate fixes how far a single write can interfere with earlier gains, whereas iteration robustness governs whether those gains survive repeated f ζ f_{\zeta} applications.
The substrate is either Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} , written by GRPO [ 65 ] , PPO [ 174 ] , or DPO [ 175 ] , or a non-parametric component such as memory [ 55 , 176 , 116 , 177 , 178 ] , tools [ 67 , 179 ] , or prompts [ 180 , 181 , 182 ] .
Three documented failure modes threaten iteration robustness: compounding reward hacking through accumulated Stage 2 bias within single runs [ 9 , 129 ] , distribution shift as the rollout law leaves the calibration region of V ν V_{\nu} [ 14 ] , and catastrophic forgetting through interference in Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} [ 95 , 161 ] .
Hacking and shift enter across the cycle boundary, whereas forgetting arises inside f ζ f_{\zeta} and yields to experience replay, parameter isolation, and regularized updates [ 95 , 161 , 183 , 184 , 185 , 186 ] .
Within one task family, the three modes can compound into long-cycle drift , a slow multi-source degradation that terminal-only reporting leaves undetected and per-round scores on a fixed benchmark suite reveal only as non-monotone curves. Generalization Constraint. Even with high S ζ S_{\zeta} , a persisted update confers no broader benefit if it improves only the goals in 𝒢 k \mathcal{G}_{k} , UC thus remaining useful only while 𝒜 k + 1 \mathcal{A}_{k+1} improves on a non-trivial share of the goals outside 𝒢 k \mathcal{G}_{k} . Transfer locus fixes where a write takes effect: on any goal that activates the affected weights for parameters, upon retrieval for memory, upon invocation for tools, and within the templating scope for prompts. Experience coverage determines the subspace of 𝒢 \mathcal{G} to which the update can generalize, and reported gains stay mostly within the support of 𝒟 k \mathcal{D}_{k} , as the fixed loops UI-Voyager [ 187 ] , UI-Mem [ 104 ] , and an online skill library [ 164 ] train and evaluate on the same task sets.
Achieved scope rises from in-distribution transfer to unsampled goals of the same regime, through cross-task transfer to neighboring goal subspaces, to cross-domain transfer to other GUI platforms or real hardware, the last reached by few methods on small trial counts [ 166 , 18 ] .
The substrate thus upper-bounds transfer, and the coverage of 𝒟 k \mathcal{D}_{k} sets how much of it a cycle realizes. Within-Regime Specialization. Neither factor of Eq. 11 has a scenario-fixed ceiling, and UC therefore specializes within each quadrant, not along an axis. S ζ S_{\zeta} binds when FA is gameable or EG is non-recoverable, and G ζ G_{\zeta} binds when 𝒟 k \mathcal{D}_{k} covers too little of 𝒢 \mathcal{G} .
Shared substrates therefore do not imply identical bottlenecks.
Multimodal reasoning and GUI/Web navigation share Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} as the dominant substrate ( Table V ), yet reasoning sustains intensive parametric writes under low-cost EG and rule-checkable FA and is bounded by long-cycle drift [ 5 , 22 , 64 ] , while navigation stabilizes its parametric writes by trajectory replay [ 14 , 17 ] and, in both parametric and training-free forms, is bounded by transfer that stays within one platform.
Multimodal generation shares the retention bottleneck through compounding hacking of its parametric writes [ 9 , 129 ] , and embodied manipulation shares the generalization bottleneck, writing to Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} and to skills, memory, and harnesses [ 188 , 189 , 190 , 166 ] within single simulator suites.
Few methods measure either factor across cycles, and retention on earlier-round goals is measured in a single self-evolving loop, RSI in UMMs [ 191 ] , besides fixed-loop continual reinforcement learning [ 95 , 161 ] .
UC thus inherits its bottleneck from the first two stages and refines a method within its quadrant without moving it. K ¯ \bar{K} F ¯ \bar{F} Fig. 3 : Cross-scenario positioning matrix. The axes are the scenario-fixed ceilings K ¯ \bar{K} and F ¯ \bar{F} , both decreasing away from the origin. Each scenario sits at the inferred height of its ceilings, with shading deepening from MR (neither binding) to EM (both binding). The MG, GW, and EM cells denote open-ended prompts, sandboxed environments with state scripts, and physical deployment, and dashed arrows show the displacement of closed-form generation sub-families, of navigation on live websites, and of simulated manipulation. III-E Cross-Scenario Positioning The stage analyses locate where each scenario’s loop is bound, and this subsection condenses them into a positioning matrix that accounts for the cross-scenario asymmetry.
To serve as an axis, a stage factor must be scenario-exogenous , fixed by task structure instead of algorithmic choice, and substitution-resistant , bounded in a way that the paired factor of its stage cannot relax.
Realized values, which each method sets through its budget and evaluator, fail the first condition, leaving the ceilings as the only admissible axes. Axis Selection. We select the ceilings K ¯ \bar{K} and F ¯ \bar{F} as the two axes, the only stage factors satisfying both conditions. The horizontal axis K ¯ \bar{K} is fixed by interaction cost and recoverability, and no information-gain intervention lifts it. With B B shared as a comparison convention, not a measured quantity, K ¯ \bar{K} orders scenarios by 𝔼 τ ​ [ c ​ ( τ ) ] \mathbb{E}_{\tau}[c(\tau)] . The vertical axis F ¯ \bar{F} is fixed by verifier availability, and no localization refinement raises it. We assess F ¯ \bar{F} on the task families that define a scenario’s target, such as open-ended prompts in generation and open task sets on hardware in manipulation, excluding sub-families whose closed-form success predicates push the ceiling near one. Its value is the highest fidelity a reported evaluator attains on these families, an estimate that may run high, agreement measured on fixed outputs tending to fall under optimization. The other four factors each fail a condition: the information gain fails scenario-exogeneity, its ceiling sup ω 𝔼 ⁡ [ I 𝒜 k ] \sup_{\omega}\mathbb{E}[I_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}] being set by backbone coverage; the localization L ν L_{\nu} fails substitution-resistance in that a higher F ν F_{\nu} offsets its deficit when an unbiased anchor exists; and S ζ S_{\zeta} and G ζ G_{\zeta} have no scenario-fixed ceiling. TABLE VI : Cross-cycle degradation modes. Each failure mode is characterized by its mechanism, the stage where it surfaces, the path that carries it, and where it is primarily mitigated, all inferred from within-run, fixed-loop, and out-of-loop evidence. Failure mode Cross-cycle mechanism Surfaces at Carried by Primary mitigation locus ①Compounding reward hacking An FA fidelity deficit inside V ν V_{\nu} ’s calibration support, tolerable per cycle, re-amplifies into a directional bias UC Agent edge, into FA if V ν V_{\nu} reads 𝒜 k \mathcal{A}_{k} Tighten F ν F_{\nu} at FA, or shrink the update step ②Distribution shift Rollouts drift off V ν V_{\nu} ’s calibration support, where its errors are extrapolations FA Agent edge, environment path Recalibrate V ν V_{\nu} at FA, or resample in EG ③Forgetting and plasticity loss A moving goal distribution makes writes to Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} interfere, erasing earlier skills and eroding plasticity [ 192 ] UC Environment path, goal edge Replay earlier goals in EG, or isolate or reset parameters ④Curriculum collapse σ ω \sigma_{\omega} contracts the goal support toward mastered regions, causing 𝔼 ⁡ [ I 𝒜 k ] \mathbb{E}[I_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}] to decay EG Goal edge, or agent edge into σ ω \sigma_{\omega} Inject exploration into σ ω \sigma_{\omega} , or score goals by difficulty ⑤Model collapse Self-generated training data narrows π 𝒜 k \pi_{\mathcal{A}_{k}} and erases its tails, driving 𝔼 ⁡ [ I 𝒜 k ] \mathbb{E}[I_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}] down under any sampler [ 193 ] EG Agent edge Accumulate real or external data instead of replacing it [ 194 ] Quadrant Assignment. We place each scenario in a quadrant of Figure 3 by the height of its two ceilings, calling a ceiling binding when it is low. Multimodal reasoning faces neither low ceiling, multimodal generation a low F ¯ \bar{F} on open-ended prompts, GUI/Web navigation a low K ¯ \bar{K} under latency-bound episodes in sandboxed environments whose end states per-task scripts inspect, and embodied manipulation both in physical deployment, its target.
Three displacements, drawn dashed in the matrix, qualify this placement: ( i ) most embodied loops learn in simulation, where success predicates raise F ¯ \bar{F} and group sampling eases K ¯ \bar{K} , shifting the operating point toward the navigation cell; ( ii ) generation contains verifiable sub-families such as OCR-scored prompts [ 195 , 196 , 197 , 198 , 199 ] and program-rendered charts [ 191 ] , raising F ¯ \bar{F} toward the reasoning cell; and ( iii ) navigation on live websites or free-form goals lacks state scripts and falls to screenshot judges [ 101 , 200 , 201 ] , lowering F ¯ \bar{F} toward the manipulation cell.
The four scenarios thus occupy four quadrants, consistent with the bottom rows of Tables III and IV , and the two off-diagonal cells, cheap but gameable generation and costly but verifiable navigation, are combinations that a single difficulty ordering would merge. In the absence of any study that estimates either ceiling under one definition in more than one scenario, the placement remains inferred from reported operating points. Backbone Prior as a Confound. The backbone 𝒜 0 \mathcal{A}_{0} confounds this placement by bounding what a loop can reach independently of task structure. In text-only models, self-verification sharpens a model toward outputs it already ranks highly [ 202 ] , reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards narrows the problems solved at large sampling budgets [ 203 ] , and gains from spurious rewards vary with the model family [ 204 ] . Backbones also differ across scenarios, from vision-language models pretrained on large question-answering corpora in reasoning to action policies with far less motor pretraining in manipulation. Part of the contrast that the matrix attributes to K ¯ \bar{K} and F ¯ \bar{F} may thus reflect backbone coverage, a factor that stays entangled with task structure. Feasibility Filter. The matrix supplies the first of two checks on whether a mechanism transfers across scenarios. Scenario-fixed by construction, the two ceilings bound the rollouts and the fidelity open to any learning loop within a quadrant. A transferred mechanism further presupposes resources of its source scenario that neither ceiling records, such as output equivalence or checkable outcomes, and where the target lacks them, the mechanism is conditional on an adaptation or a sub-family that supplies them. The matrix thus bounds within-cycle feasibility without deciding portability, its verdicts remaining untested while each reported comparison stays confined to one scenario. III-F Cross-Cycle Coupling Beyond the matrix’s within-cycle bounds, the recurrence of Eq. 5 couples successive cycles.
It feeds two cycle- k k outputs into Stage 1 of cycle k + 1 k{+}1 , the updated agent 𝒜 k + 1 \mathcal{A}_{k+1} that induces the next rollout policy and the extended history ℋ k + 1 \mathcal{H}_{k+1} that conditions the next goal draw, each defining a directed edge across the cycle boundary.
Environmental drift, which Sec. II-A holds fixed but live websites exhibit [ 200 , 205 ] , enters as a third path from outside the loop.
These paths carry both self-reference and long-run degradation. Two Coupling Edges. The two edges differ in carrier and conditionality. The agent edge runs from Stage 3 of cycle k k to Stage 1 of cycle k + 1 k{+}1 , carrying the persisted update into 𝒜 k + 1 \mathcal{A}_{k+1} and hence into the rollout distribution of 𝒟 k + 1 \mathcal{D}_{k+1} . Present in every autonomous learning loop, it supplies the closure on which 𝒞 4 \mathcal{C}_{4} rests and extends to any further component that reads the current agent: to σ ω \sigma_{\omega} under a trained proposer [ 22 , 5 ] , to Stage 2 under an evaluator sharing Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} [ 22 , 6 ] , rendering F ν ( k ) F_{\nu}^{(k)} endogenous, and to Stage 3 under a generative model that records lessons or skills [ 20 , 162 ] . The goal edge runs from Stages 1 and 2 of cycle k k to Stage 1 of cycle k + 1 k{+}1 through σ ω ​ ( k + 1 , ℋ k + 1 ) \sigma_{\omega}(k{+}1,\mathcal{H}_{k+1}) , carrying the cycle- k k trajectories and their scores into the next goal draw. A stationary or scheduled sampler, reading no cycle-specific signal, leaves this edge inert, whereas an adaptive sampler activates it and superimposes a second feedback loop on the agent edge. Self-reference thus acts through the extensions of the agent edge and through the goal edge: a fixed-loop method retains only the agent edge into Stage 1, and a frozen snapshot satisfies self-reference without opening any of these paths. Five Degradation Modes. We interpret long-run degradation through these paths under a mitigation-locality reading , in which the stage where a degradation surfaces, the path that carries it, and the stage where it is best mitigated need not coincide, and Table VI records all three for five modes. Compounding reward hacking and distribution shift travel along the agent edge as the in-support and extrapolation variants of Goodhart’s law [ 206 ] , which reward-model overoptimization exhibits together [ 207 ] , with shift also entering along the environment path [ 14 , 200 ] . Forgetting and plasticity loss follow a moving goal distribution carried by the environment path or the goal edge [ 192 ] , and the two collapse modes contract the support of EG, curriculum collapse through the goal edge or the agent edge into σ ω \sigma_{\omega} [ 131 , 5 ] and model collapse through the agent edge under any sampler [ 193 ] . The three loci diverge most for hacking and forgetting, which both surface at UC yet admit remedies upstream of the symptom, a tighter F ν F_{\nu} at FA [ 9 ] and replay in EG. Long-cycle drift compounds the first three modes within one task family and forms no sixth mode. The agent-edge extension into UC, absent from the table, surfaces through erroneous writes, within a single pass in Mobile-Agent-E [ 162 ] and across later rollouts in SEAR [ 20 ] . With existing loops running only a few rounds, these mechanisms are inferred from within-run and fixed-loop evidence, and the reading stops at the cycle boundary. Scenario Exposure. The scenarios engage these paths unequally. In reasoning, a learned proposer extends the agent edge into σ ω \sigma_{\omega} and pass-rate curricula open the goal edge [ 208 , 209 ] , both inviting curriculum collapse. Reasoning and generation both extend the agent edge into FA through self-referential verifiers, yet the outcome follows F ¯ \bar{F} : under reasoning’s high ceiling, F ν ( k ) F_{\nu}^{(k)} drifts below F ¯ \bar{F} , whereas under generation’s low ceiling, compounding hacking sets in unless a frozen judge cuts that extension. In navigation and manipulation, task or environment changes drive shift and forgetting along the environment path and agent edge, and costly rollouts limit the EG resampling that counters them. Coupling thus extends the asymmetry across cycles. IV Self-Evolution in Multimodal Reasoning Multimodal reasoning occupies the quadrant of Figure 3 in which neither ceiling is low, its rollouts being single decoding passes and its short answers admitting exact match against a pre-collected or computed reference.
Training against pre-collected keys under a stationary sampler forms a fixed loop, from which self-evolving methods depart along two branches.
Weight-updating loops mostly pose their own questions or train on unlabeled ones, letting the agent’s outputs supply most answers and tying the evaluator to the model it trains, which makes the realized fidelity F ν ( k ) F_{\nu}^{(k)} , not F ¯ \bar{F} , the binding factor.
Training-free loops keep a frozen VLM with the key or a program and let the agent write the skills or tools it reuses, several gating each write against a validation reference, moving the cost of self-reference to the frozen writer.
Reported loops run within one training run, over up to nine rounds of weight updates or sixteen of training-free writes, or over one pass of a benchmark stream. Methods are grouped by whether the agent generates questions and visual inputs, and each loop component is tagged by its realizing party: a agent, b bootstrap, and e environment or task generation.

The task families center on visual question answering over mathematical, chart, and diagram inputs [ 240 , 241 , 242 ] , extending to pairwise quality judgments [ 211 ] , temporally grounded video questions [ 160 , 127 ] , metric spatial questions over 3D scenes [ 235 ] , and physical dynamics in videos [ 239 , 226 , 209 ] .
These goals come from a dataset, a fixed generator, or generative roles of the agent, and in weight-updating loops the solver typically answers each goal in K k K_{k} independent decoding passes, occasionally with tool calls, yielding a 𝒟 k \mathcal{D}_{k} that groups answers to the same question, whereas training-free loops run the frozen VLM on each goal with its current skills, tools, or notes. Table VII summarizes the self-evolving methods that satisfy the conditions of Sec. II-C . Quantity and Quality Constraints. Experience generation is bound by quality at saturation ( Table III ), the task structure leaving quantity slack.
A rollout is a single decoding pass over a static input, an episode with T = 1 T\!=\!1 except where tool calls precede the answer [ 216 , 232 , 228 , 225 , 220 , 224 ] , and leaves no side effect to revert, keeping both interaction cost and recoverability from lowering K ¯ \bar{K} .
Information gain, in contrast, arises in single-answer episodes only from goals whose pass rate under the current solver lies strictly between zero and one.
A dataset or a fixed generator bounds the supply of such goals by how many lie near the edge of the current agent’s competence, a number that depends on the backbone’s pretraining and, by the mechanism that stalls self-play [ 131 ] , shrinks as the agent masters these goals. Self-Posed Goals. Larger groups do not appear to substitute for informative goals under self-consistency rewards, which lose contrast as groups grow, and loops raise the information gain instead by letting the agent pose its goals or by reweighting goals by pass rate, at a cost paid in feedback.
Reported group sizes stay between four and ten rollouts per goal despite cheap decoding passes [ 7 , 216 , 6 , 22 , 8 , 218 , 234 , 126 , 127 , 232 , 215 ] , and EvoLMM finds its self-consistency reward best at four to six samples, reporting over-consensus collapse at twelve [ 22 ] , consistent with the reward, not the cost, limiting K k K_{k} .
The agent enters σ ω \sigma_{\omega} either as a generative role within Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} that synthesizes the question beside the solver, and in some loops the visual input, or through a rule that reweights dataset or generator goals by the solver’s pass rate, alone in SyncLoop [ 208 ] or on top of a learned proposer [ 237 , 239 , 209 ] .
Proposer-solver loops after R-Zero [ 243 ] reward goals of intermediate solver agreement through terms that vanish with p g ​ ( 1 − p g ) p_{g}(1-p_{g}) [ 234 , 126 ] , adding diversity terms against a narrowing of goals [ 127 , 5 ] .
These rewards are computed in FA yet reshape the goal distribution of EG.
In VisPlay, a trained Questioner lifts Qwen2.5-VL-3B from 30.61 to 44.16 after one round against 33.77 for an untrained Challenger [ 5 ] , a comparison that attributes about three quarters of the gain to learning the role.
The gain is paid in feedback, where a self-posed question lacks a key unless a program computes its answer, leaving the answer to the agent’s own outputs. IV-B FA Bound by Realized Fidelity Each cycle hands feedback acquisition groups of short answers to the same question, or the tool-calling episodes of a frozen agent, and the loops score each answer against one or more of three references ( Table VII ).
The agent’s own outputs ( a ), through a majority vote, a self-judgment, the proposer’s answer, or consistency under image transformations, score self-posed or unlabeled questions [ 22 , 5 , 23 ] .
An answer computed by task generation ( e ) scores questions whose answers programs, game rules, a geometric environment, or a physics engine determine [ 64 , 218 , 235 , 239 ] , and a pre-collected key ( b ) scores labeled sets [ 221 , 223 , 225 ] .
Weight-updating methods mostly turn scores into group-normalized rewards for GRPO-style updates or into preference pairs for DPO [ 7 , 5 , 213 , 214 ] , whereas training-free methods use them to admit skills, tools, or notes into a library [ 146 , 225 , 222 ] . Fidelity and Localization Constraints. Feedback acquisition is bound by realized fidelity ( Table IV ), its attainable fidelity being high and localization imposing little.
A short answer admits exact match against a reference fixed outside the agent and leaves no channel unchecked, and without a key the program-computed answers of VQS stay correct on 94.4% of retained questions among 500 human-audited ones [ 64 ] , a lower bound on F ¯ \bar{F} under the conversion of Sec. III-C .
Consensus already falls below this bound within one round, with 24% of retained majority-vote labels and 18% of retained model-judge labels wrong in the same audit [ 64 ] .
Across rounds, externally estimated pseudo-label accuracy falls from 72.0 to 61.0 over three iterations in VisPlay [ 5 ] and from 64.5 to 37.5 over five in Video-Zero [ 160 ] , declines that bear on F ν ( k ) F_{\nu}^{(k)} only jointly with rising question difficulty, to which VisPlay attributes its own.
Gaming resistance of the vote under long optimization remains untested here, whereas prolonged training against a majority-vote self-reward in text-only reasoning ends in reward hacking and abrupt collapse [ 244 ] .
Single-answer episodes fix L ν = 1 L_{\nu}\!=\!1 , the step-level agreement of iReasoner refining credit only within the output [ 170 ] , and only the short tool-integrated episodes of Agent0-VL, scored as a whole [ 216 ] , leave credit coarser. Vote Preconditions and Evaluator Drift. With localization free, every deficit of feedback acquisition is a fidelity deficit, and self-derived rewards make that fidelity a property of the model being trained.
A vote recovers the model’s belief and tracks R R only where answers admit an equivalence relation and the pretrained prior concentrates on the correct answer [ 80 , 132 ] , discrete answers supplying the first condition and the backbone, not the task structure, deciding the second.
Within these conditions the reward form matters, TTRV lowering accuracy on four of five VQA benchmarks under a hard majority-vote reward and raising it on all five under a frequency-proportional reward with entropy control [ 171 ] .
The evaluators further differ in whether they move with the policy: votes over current samples, parameter-sharing supervisors [ 126 ] , and self-judging models [ 214 ] keep the agent edge into FA, judges frozen at the initial model cut it [ 212 , 230 ] , and the moving-average verifier of Trinity lags the policy by construction [ 231 ] .
A frozen evaluator still shares the prior of the policies descended from it, removing drift but not a fixed bias, and reports under differing numbers of rounds leave the designs uncompared.
Answers fixed outside the agent, by a key or a program, restore F ¯ \bar{F} only for labeled sets and program-computable families, the same restriction that experience generation imposes on the goals. IV-C UC Bound by Retention Update consolidation in multimodal reasoning persists the scored experience in two forms, weight updates and training-free writes ( Table VII ).
Weight-updating methods write Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} with an update rule fixed before the loop, through group-relative policy gradients, preference optimization, or fine-tuning, either once per round over two to nine reported rounds [ 5 , 160 , 64 , 235 , 238 , 209 , 214 ] or continuously within a single training run.
Training-free methods keep a frozen VLM and let the agent write text or code into skill and tool libraries [ 146 , 223 , 225 , 220 , 227 ] , memory cards or notes [ 219 , 222 , 226 ] , or harnesses of tools, skills, and execution code [ 224 , 233 ] , over benchmark streams or, in VisualEvolve, up to sixteen rounds, whereas a separate o4-mini abstracts the tool library of TVP [ 217 ] .
MetaForge alone combines the two, registering validated tools that its policy forges during GRPO into a persistent library [ 228 ] . Retention and Generalization Constraints. Update consolidation is bound by retention ( Table V ), inferred from non-monotone curves, not a measured loss, whereas generalization, though reported, is confounded with the backbone prior.
No method measures retention on earlier-round goals, and the closest evidence, per-round scores on a fixed benchmark suite, traces the increment 𝒥 k + 1 − 𝒥 k \mathcal{J}_{k+1}-\mathcal{J}_{k} of Eq. 6 over the evaluation distribution, not the trained goals.
These curves rise monotonically in Agent0-VL [ 216 ] but peak or dip mid-run elsewhere [ 8 , 5 ] , while single benchmarks reverse more often, MM-Vet falling from 48.62 to 38.07 over three VisPlay iterations as the average rises [ 5 ] .
Among weight-updating methods, only ISR-DPO and Marvo run beyond five rounds, the former gaining through its ninth iteration [ 214 ] and the latter degrading after about the fifth or sixth of eight [ 232 ] .
Parametric writes generalize more freely, reaching any goal that activates the updated weights and lifting standard benchmarks after training on synthesized questions [ 7 , 5 , 22 ] .
These gains are consistent with elicitation of the backbone prior, most weight-updating methods resting on Qwen2.5-VL or Qwen3-VL, the family whose text-only counterparts gain even under spurious rewards [ 204 ] .
V-Zero offers a partial control, reaching 51.9 against 50.8 for supervised GRPO on the same Qwen2.5-VL-7B backbone [ 7 ] , and only an equal-budget non-evolving baseline would separate self-evolution from elicitation.
Non-parametric writes act only when retrieved, and their transfer is tested directly, EvoSpatial evaluating libraries frozen after one benchmark on 18 others [ 220 ] and VideoEvoHarness moving an evolved harness to a second backbone [ 233 ] . Reference-Gated Writes. Consolidation inherits its exposure from the first two stages, and the two forms of writing differ in how they guard against it.
Where the evaluator moves with the policy or a proposer supplies the goals, a parametric write is scored and driven by quantities that change with the update itself, and the update rule accepts every scored step without a fixed reference that could reveal a later round erasing an earlier gain.
Several training-free methods gate their writes against such a reference, admitting a skill or change only on a validation or held-out gain [ 146 , 233 ] , promoting a skill-tool pair only on a strict gain over its training sample [ 225 ] , rolling back note edits that do not raise batch accuracy [ 222 ] , or selecting snapshots on fixed validation or development sets [ 223 , 224 ] , whereas TVP checks a new tool only against the outputs of the programs it replaces [ 217 ] .
A guard on retention thus exists only where gated training-free writes keep a fixed reference. IV-D Limits and Preconditions Bottleneck Attribution. The binding bottlenecks of feedback and consolidation arise from self-reference, not from task structure.
Both ceilings are slack for any learning loop, and a fixed loop that trains against pre-collected keys, as M-STAR does [ 152 ] , scores its rollouts at the fidelity of an exact match.
The weight-updating branch gives up this reference to pose and answer its own questions, its answers coming from the agent’s own outputs, whose realized fidelity falls below F ¯ \bar{F} , and its parametric writes lacking a fixed reference against which a later round could be checked.
The training-free branch keeps the key or a validation set and admits self-reference only through the update, shifting the cost to the writer, whose frozen backbone both diagnoses a bottleneck and writes its remedy, its capability bounding both, a limitation that Dynamo reports for itself [ 225 ] .
Self-reference repays part of this cost in experience generation, where a learned proposer relieves the saturation of fixed goal supplies and accounts for most of the first-round gain of VisPlay [ 5 ] .
The few cross-round audits report declines confounded with rising question difficulty, and a large question-answering prior in the backbones may account for part of the gains in both branches, disqualifying the least constrained cell as a clean baseline for the other scenarios.
This attribution rests on the stage evidence and on the provenance of each method in Table VII , not on a controlled comparison with fixed loops. Cross-Cycle Degradation. Across cycles, self-reference exposes multimodal reasoning to degradations that a fixed loop with keys avoids.
On the goal side, learned proposers extend the agent edge into σ ω \sigma_{\omega} and pass-rate curricula open the goal edge [ 208 , 237 , 239 , 209 ] , both inviting curriculum collapse.
On the feedback side, self-derived rewards extend the agent edge into FA, inviting compounding reward hacking, and training on self-labeled answers invites model collapse along the edge into the rollout policy.
The extension of training-free writers into UC remains untested, and cross-round evidence reduces to the Vision-Zero plateau, where a self-derived signal stops informing updates [ 218 ] , and the late-round decline of Marvo [ 232 ] , with per-round reversals of single benchmarks fitting hacking and forgetting alike. Presupposed Resources. The mechanisms of multimodal reasoning presuppose resources that its short, discrete answers supply.
Majority votes and agreement-based proposer rewards presuppose answers that admit an equivalence relation [ 22 , 234 , 126 ] , program-computed answers presuppose a program that determines the answer at task generation [ 64 ] , and reference-gated writes presuppose a key or validation set held outside the writer [ 146 , 225 ] . V Self-Evolution in Multimodal Generation Multimodal generation occupies the quadrant of Figure 3 in which only the fidelity ceiling is low, an open-ended image or video having no reference output to be checked against outside sub-families such as object counts, rendered text, or program-drawn charts.
Training against learned preference scorers fixed before the loop forms a fixed loop that this ceiling already binds, such scorers being exploitable under optimization [ 245 , 129 ] .
Weight-updating loops mostly derive the verifier from the agent, leaving the ceiling unchanged but letting the verifier’s bias move with the policy and exposing the loop to compounding reward hacking.
Training-free loops mostly let a frozen backbone judge its own outputs and write its memory or skills, sharing one bias between judge and writer.
Reported loops run within one training run, over at most five rounds, or over one pass of a task stream. V-A EG Bound by Checkable Prompts In multimodal generation, experience generation draws prompts as goals, paired with a source image in editing, and the outputs are images, videos, 3D scenes, charts, diagrams, or educational animations.
The task families center on compositional text-to-image prompts [ 128 , 12 , 11 ] , extending to aesthetic prompt optimization [ 246 , 247 ] , text rendering [ 245 ] , safety [ 248 ] , charts [ 191 ] , text-to-video generation [ 249 , 250 ] , image editing [ 251 , 252 ] , and diagrams, 3D scenes, method illustrations, and animations built through tool chains [ 253 , 254 , 255 , 256 ] .
These goals come from a fixed pool or from generative roles of the agent, and weight-updating loops sample K k K_{k} outputs per prompt in one pass each, whereas training-free loops run a frozen agent through a workflow of tool calls, renders, and edits. Table IX summarizes the self-evolving methods that satisfy the conditions of Sec. II-C . Quantity and Quality Constraints. Experience generation is bound by quality at the coverage of checkable prompts ( Table III ), the task structure leaving quantity slack.
A rollout costs one sampling pass or an offline tool chain [ 103 ] and leaves no side effect to revert, with reported group sizes of three to sixteen samples per prompt [ 128 , 129 , 13 , 9 , 257 , 245 ] .
Information gain, in contrast, runs out at the edge of what a check can score, not at the edge of the agent’s competence as in multimodal reasoning.
Fixed pools come from benchmark categories whose attributes a detector, OCR, or a VQA model scores, such as those of GenEval and T2I-CompBench [ 128 , 129 , 11 , 10 ] , and a learning loop drives pass rates on such a pool toward one, where p g ​ ( 1 − p g ) p_{g}(1-p_{g}) approaches zero, as the fixed loop Flow-GRPO raises SD3.5-M from 0.63 to 0.95 on GenEval [ 195 ] .
Open-ended prompts would still separate good outputs from poor ones under R R , yet no check fixed before the loop scores them, leaving the loop unable to draw information from them.
Loops drawing every round from one prompt distribution report diminishing gains by about the third round, consistent with this saturation [ 12 , 248 ] . Checkable Goal Composition. A larger group adds samples of a prompt without extending the checkable pool, and loops raise 𝔼 ⁡ [ I 𝒜 k ] \mathbb{E}[I_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}] instead by letting the agent compose or reselect goals, at a cost paid in fidelity once the goals leave what a fixed check scores.
Built-in checks keep composed goals within checkable sub-families, fixing answers at prompt construction in UniRL [ 128 ] , decomposing each prompt into yes/no questions in SILMM [ 12 ] , and restricting the specifications that RSI in UMMs plans from its own failures to charts that program execution verifies [ 191 ] .
Goals beyond these sub-families fall to frozen external judges [ 258 , 259 , 260 ] , whereas ASG poses understanding questions over unlabeled images, scored by the agreement of its own answers across framings at a fidelity that moves with the policy [ 40 ] , and reselection stays within the pool, InternalGap regenerating the prompts that yielded no accepted image [ 11 ] .
Controlled returns are reported only for built-in checks and external judges, RSI in UMMs reaching 59.0% on charts after four rounds against 46.3% for longer training on its original data and leading a fixed distribution of equally many verified charts in every round [ 191 ] , and RSIgen gaining 4.7, 3.1, and 2.2 points over curricula compiled from frozen sources across its three attempts [ 260 ] . Experience Generation (EG) Feedback Acquisition (FA) Update Consolidation (UC) Method 𝝈 \boldsymbol{\sigma} Goal content 𝑲 𝒌 \boldsymbol{K_{k}} 𝑽 \boldsymbol{V} Scoring reference & signal 𝒇 \boldsymbol{f} Update Rounds Backbone RSIgen [ 260 ] arXiv’26 a curricula compiled from own probes – b fixed external evaluator b agent and generator updates 3 attempts BAGEL, Qwen-Image, Wan-Image OctoT2I [ 258 ] CVPR’26 a self-proposed prompts over concept dimensions 5 b frozen MLLM probability of alignment a routing knowledge base (I) 1 pass Qwen2-0.5B router GenEvolve [ 266 ] arXiv’26 b prompts with judge-extracted experience 6 b Gemini KScore judge, program-sufficiency judge a GRPO, self-distillation from own experience-conditioned targets – Qwen3-VL-8B, Qwen-Image-Edit V-B FA Bound by Attainable Fidelity Each cycle hands feedback acquisition a group of outputs for the same prompt or the renders of one workflow, and the loops score each output as a whole ( Table IX ).
The scores come from the agent itself ( a ), through the generator’s confidence, its understanding branch, a lagged, snapshot, or co-trained judge, or the frozen backbone that runs the workflow, or from outside it, through an external judge ( b ) or program execution ( e ).
Weight-updating methods mostly turn scores into group-normalized rewards for GRPO-style updates or into preference pairs for DPO [ 9 , 129 , 13 , 12 , 246 ] , whereas training-free methods receive verbal critiques, checklist scores, or pass/fail verdicts [ 254 , 253 , 264 , 103 ] that trigger repairs within an episode and gate persistent writes. Fidelity and Localization Constraints. Feedback acquisition is bound by the fidelity ceiling ( Table IV ), to which signal source, channel coverage, and gaming resistance each contribute, while localization imposes little.
An image admits no equivalence check against a fixed reference, leaving learned scorers and model judges, whose agreement with humans on open-ended content can turn negative, the frozen reviewer of ManimAgent correlating with blind ratings of 37 educational videos at r = − 0.17 r\!=\!-0.17 [ 256 ] .
Instruments fixed before the loop score parts of the objective, such as detectors, OCR, and PickScore in Flow-GRPO [ 195 ] and HPS-v2.1, CLIP, and VideoAlign in DanceGRPO [ 267 ] , and Flow-GRPO raises PickScore and OCR accuracy while human raters judge its images below the base model in quality [ 245 ] .
Under optimization, IRIS attributes the realism drop of its HPSv2-trained baseline to reward hacking [ 129 ] .
Only sub-families with a closed-form predicate escape the ceiling, RSI in UMMs gaining more on charts from execution-verified data than from the model’s own best-rated images [ 191 ] .
Localization stays near L ν = 1 L_{\nu}\!=\!1 , a single sampling pass forming one action and tool-orchestrated systems scoring renders with execution errors as their chief step-level signal [ 256 , 103 ] . Verifier Dependence on the Policy. With localization nearly free, every deficit of feedback acquisition is a fidelity deficit, and the methods differ in how far the verifier depends on the policy it scores.
A verifier that depends on the current policy keeps the agent edge into FA, letting F ν ( k ) F_{\nu}^{(k)} move with each update and inviting compounding reward hacking, whereas weaker dependence slows or cuts that edge and leaves a bias fixed across cycles.
Generator confidence depends on the policy entirely, and SOLACE and IRIS disagree even on its sign, rewarding higher denoising confidence and lower self-certainty, respectively [ 9 , 129 ] .
Understanding-branch checks hold only the prompt fixed and substitute for an external verifier only if the judging parameters are separated from the judged ones, the two directions are symmetric, and understanding exceeds generation.
Shared weights violate the first condition, which KL penalties to a reference policy slow but do not remove [ 10 , 128 , 40 , 13 ] , the non-inverse relation between text-to-image and image-to-text weakens the second behind reverse-likelihood rewards [ 10 , 257 , 261 ] , and the third, the internal gap that InternalGap converts into supervision [ 11 ] , remains untested within a run [ 10 ] .
Weaker dependence comes from co-evolving or lagged verifiers [ 246 , 263 , 245 , 249 , 262 ] , from frozen self-verifiers [ 247 , 251 ] , including frozen backbones that also write the update [ 256 , 264 , 254 ] , and from external or programmatic verifiers [ 259 , 260 , 191 ] , and no report compares these designs at a matched number of rounds.
Such designs thus trade a moving bias for a fixed one without raising F ¯ \bar{F} . V-C UC Bound by Retention Update consolidation in multimodal generation persists the scored experience in two forms, weight updates and training-free writes ( Table IX ).
Weight-updating methods write Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} with an update rule fixed before the loop, through group-relative policy gradients, preference optimization, fine-tuning, or self-distillation, either continuously within a single training run or over two to five reported rounds [ 246 , 247 , 12 , 249 , 191 , 248 ] .
Training-free methods keep the generator and the agent frozen and let the agent write memory [ 256 , 265 , 165 , 253 , 250 ] , skill, constraint-function, concept, or middleware libraries [ 264 , 254 , 103 , 255 ] , or routing records [ 258 ] , over a task stream or up to five rounds.
ImageRSI alone combines the two, admitting experience to a library while retraining its judge and not its generator [ 262 ] . Retention and Generalization Constraints. Update consolidation is bound by retention ( Table V ), documented within runs and in RSI in UMMs over at most four rounds, whereas generalization is mostly untested and not shown to fail.
Only RSI in UMMs measures retention on earlier-round goals, exact category text on an earlier vocabulary falling from 72% to 60% once a round replaces that vocabulary and recovering when later rounds keep half of the old specifications, while general understanding, which the chart goals do not exercise, erodes by 2.6 points on MMBench [ 191 ] .
Within single runs, iteration robustness fails at the grain of optimizer steps, SOLACE degenerating into textureless images under prolonged optimization [ 9 ] and IRIS into meaningless images within hundreds of steps [ 129 ] , and across rounds, per-round scores cannot separate saturation from erosion, as in the slight slip of DreamLLM under SILMM on DPG-Bench [ 12 ] .
Generalization is tested only where a loop holds out evaluation goals [ 129 , 262 , 253 ] , which MemoGen does not [ 265 ] , and transfer is partial for both forms of writing, a memory that SIDiffAgent builds on GenAI-Bench raising DrawBench from 0.860 to 0.873 against 0.901 for in-domain memory [ 165 ] and the parametric writes of RSI in UMMs gaining only from 11.0% to 11.9% on a chart subset its authors judge too hard for open models [ 191 ] .
Understanding-branch gains also remain entangled with the prior that judge and generator share in a unified model. Self-Judged Writes. The exposure that consolidation faces comes from feedback acquisition, where the agent itself judges most writes, and the two forms of writing guard against it differently.
Where the verifier depends on the policy, a parametric write is scored by the policy itself or by a judge that follows it, and the update rule admits every rewarded step, held back only by regularizers such as KL penalties [ 10 , 128 , 40 , 9 , 129 , 13 ] .
Training-free writes pass an admission test before they persist, yet the reviewer gating of ManimAgent [ 256 ] and the no-loss check of COMFYCLAW on three held-out prompts that the agent synthesizes [ 264 ] reuse the frozen model that judged the experience, protecting retention on that model’s scale but not against its bias.
Only ImageRSI, through paired validation on separate calibration and holdout splits [ 262 ] , and Drawing Middleware Evolution, through rollback against reference illustrations [ 255 ] , test writes outside that model.
Retention is thus protected only where a training-free write is tested outside the model that judged it, a condition that the key- or validation-gated writes of multimodal reasoning meet more often. V-D Limits and Preconditions Bottleneck Attribution. In multimodal generation, the fidelity ceiling binds before self-reference, which changes the form of the deficit and leaves the ceiling in place.
The fixed loops of the scenario, Flow-GRPO [ 195 ] and DanceGRPO [ 267 ] among them, already train against exploitable proxies [ 129 ] , unlike the key-scored fixed loops of multimodal reasoning [ 152 ] .
Weight-updating loops replace a proxy whose bias stays put with a policy-dependent verifier whose bias moves with the policy [ 9 , 129 ] , and training-free loops let the frozen model that judges an output also write the update, judge and writer sharing one bias.
Goal selection is where self-reference earns back part of this cost, controlled returns being reported there, whereas The Image as Its Own Reward offers the only comparison of a moving verifier with a fixed one, by human preference after one run [ 245 ] .
Both the ceiling and its cost rest on stage evidence and the provenance in Table IX , not on a controlled comparison. Cross-Cycle Degradation. Across cycles, self-reference opens paths along which degradation follows the agent, with only one degradation measured across rounds.
On the goal side, learned proposers extend the agent edge into σ ω \sigma_{\omega} , and the regeneration of failed prompts in InternalGap [ 11 ] and the pruning of prompts by pass rate in OctoT2I [ 258 ] open the goal edge, both inviting curriculum collapse.
The goal edge also carries this one measured degradation, the forgetting in RSI in UMMs following a vocabulary change that a rule reading the agent’s OCR score triggers [ 191 ] .
On the feedback side, self-verifiers extend the agent edge into FA, inviting compounding reward hacking, observed only within single runs [ 9 , 129 ] , and training on self-generated images invites model collapse, which the narrowing of IRIS outputs under higher image self-certainty resembles without isolating [ 129 ] .
Curriculum collapse, the extension into UC, and cross-round self-verifier fidelity remain untested. Presupposed Resources. The mechanisms of multimodal generation presuppose resources that only part of its outputs supply.
Understanding-branch checks presuppose an output that the agent’s own perception can process and an understanding branch stronger than its generation [ 10 , 11 ] , built-in checks and execution-verified goals presuppose a sub-family with a closed-form predicate [ 128 , 191 ] , and admission gates presuppose held-out splits or reference sets outside the judging model [ 262 , 255 ] . VI Self-Evolution in GUI and Web Navigation GUI and Web navigation occupies the quadrant of Figure 3 in which only the quantity ceiling is low, each rollout being a multi-step episode that pays environment latency at every step, whereas the inspectable end state of an application or a web page keeps the attainable fidelity high in sandboxed environments, where per-task scripts read it.
Much automated online learning here runs as fixed loops, whose tasks and verifiers come from external models or benchmark authors and never read the agent’s record [ 101 , 130 , 200 , 268 , 269 , 270 , 271 , 272 , 273 , 274 , 275 , 276 , 277 ] .
Weight-updating loops allocate scarce rollouts by proposing goals or reweighting a fixed task set by its success record, and buy step-level credit with verifiers refit on the agent’s trajectories, whose fidelity moves with the policy.
Training-free loops keep the task set fixed and reuse each episode through memory or skills that a frozen backbone writes and judges, sharing one bias between judge and writer.
Reported loops span one training run, a few rounds, or one pass over a task stream, mostly evaluated on the task sets they train on. TABLE XI : Self-evolving methods for GUI and Web navigation. Methods are grouped by whether and how the goal sampler reads the agent’s record, and each loop component is tagged by its realizing party: a agent and b bootstrap.

library – a success check from trajectory and final screenshot s a executable API library (T) 160 per site GPT-4o, frozen VI-A EG Bound by Rollout Cost In GUI and Web navigation, experience generation draws goals that pair a natural-language instruction with the initial state of an application, desktop, or website, and each rollout is an episode of screenshots, often with an accessibility tree, and actions such as taps, typing, and scrolls, ending in success, failure, or a step limit.
The task families span mobile apps in AitW, AndroidWorld, and AndroidLab [ 14 , 17 , 135 ] , desktop software in OSWorld [ 115 , 16 , 278 ] , and web navigation in WebArena and VisualWebArena sandboxes and on live WebVoyager and Online-Mind2Web sites [ 201 , 284 , 163 ] .
These goals come from a benchmark task set, a task stream, or the agent’s own proposals, and weight-updating loops run episode groups per goal in parallel instances, whereas training-free loops run one or a few episodes in sequence with a frozen backbone. Table XI summarizes the self-evolving methods that satisfy the conditions of Sec. II-C . Quantity and Quality Constraints. Experience generation is bound by quantity at the episodes that parallel environment instances afford ( Table III ), an episode cost that the single passes of multimodal reasoning and generation do not incur.
A rollout spans up to 25 screen-action steps [ 130 ] , each paying rendering and environment latency on top of policy inference, 2.5 to 4.0 seconds per step on the virtual machines of UI-TARS-2 [ 125 ] , with DART-GUI reporting episodes that last tens of minutes [ 115 ] .
Group sizes of eight to 64 rollouts per task [ 130 , 115 , 17 , 101 ] are bought with parallel instances, up to 64 emulators in DigiRL [ 14 ] , whereas training-free loops run each task once or a few times in sequence [ 162 , 163 , 280 , 281 ] .
Quality decides what each scarce episode returns, and on long-horizon tasks the loss falls mostly on goals beyond the agent’s reach, where a binary outcome gives p g ​ ( 1 − p g ) ≈ 0 p_{g}(1-p_{g})\approx 0 , consistent with starting success rates of 11.3% on the professional software of SEAgent [ 16 ] and 18.1% on AndroidLab in UI-Genie [ 135 ] . Reuse, Reweighting, and Goal Generation. A larger group costs environment instances, and loops raise 𝔼 ⁡ [ I 𝒜 k ] \mathbb{E}[I_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}] instead along three routes, reuse, reweighting, and goal generation, which the inspectable end state keeps verifiable.
Under stationary sampling, reuse raises the return of later episodes by conditioning them on memory and skills that the agent writes [ 162 , 278 , 282 , 279 , 280 , 163 , 281 , 283 ] or by training the agent on steps that value functions or reward models fit on its rollouts select and label [ 14 , 144 ] , both lifting unreachable tasks toward informative success rates.
Reweighting by the success record keeps the verifier of a fixed task set, lowering the rollout count of tasks with high running success in DART-GUI [ 115 ] and the sampling probability of tasks that earn no reward over consecutive epochs in MobileRL [ 17 ] , or returning earlier failures [ 135 ] .
Goal generation needs a verifier for each new goal, which reads the outcome off the screen through a model or judge fixed before the loop [ 16 , 15 ] or a check that the agent itself runs [ 284 , 201 ] .
Each route carries a controlled return, and the most direct test of substitution favors reuse, EvoSkill-GUI with skills outperforming pass@3 without them on MobileWorld at a comparable token budget [ 281 ] , while removing the failure filter of MobileRL lowers its AndroidWorld success [ 17 ] and random task generation lowers ACuRL’s third-round LibreOffice Impress score from 40.7% to 36.4% [ 15 ] . VI-B FA Bound by Localization Each cycle hands feedback acquisition an episode of screens and actions, which most loops score once at its end and loops with learned step judges score per step ( Table XI ).
Scores from outside the agent ( b ) come from per-task scripts and rule-based rewards written with the benchmark [ 115 , 17 ] , the fixed evaluators of DigiRL and SEAgent [ 14 , 16 ] , and benchmark success [ 280 ] .
Scores from the agent ( a ) come from value functions and reward models fit on its trajectories [ 14 , 135 , 144 ] , the Judger role of the base model that SCALE fine-tunes in its other roles [ 284 ] , and the frozen backbone that acts and judges its own episode in training-free loops [ 162 , 278 , 279 , 163 , 281 , 201 ] .
Weight-updating methods turn scores into group-normalized advantages or rejection-sampled training targets [ 115 , 17 , 135 , 144 ] , whereas training-free methods turn them into success labels and diagnoses that gate memory and skill writes [ 278 , 281 , 163 ] . Fidelity and Localization Constraints. Feedback acquisition is bound by localization ( Table IV ), the attainable fidelity being high wherever the goal fixes an end state, though signal sources reach that ceiling unevenly.
Task completion resides in the final screen, application database, or file system, which per-task scripts and rule-based rewards inspect directly [ 115 , 17 ] , whereas model judges fall short, EvoSkill-GUI’s same-backbone critic erring on 28 of 143 audited failures and passing at least four as successes [ 281 ] .
Free-form goals and live websites lack scripts and fall to screenshot judges [ 101 , 200 , 201 ] , which see the screen but not the backend state.
Under optimization, ACuRL audits its judge against on-policy trajectories over three rounds, with precision of 89.1% to 98.0% [ 15 ] , whereas judge benchmarks score fixed trajectories [ 285 ] or the downstream success of policies trained on each judge [ 142 ] .
Localization binds every source alike, even an exact checker spreading one verdict over episodes of up to 50 actions [ 281 ] , with L ν L_{\nu} falling as episodes lengthen.
Loops recover step-level signal at a credit-assignment cost through learned value functions and judges [ 14 , 16 , 135 , 144 ] or a failure-diagnosing critic for training-free revision [ 281 ] , step-level advantages speeding DigiRL’s learning by about 12% on its Web Shopping subset [ 14 ] . Localization Bought with Fidelity. With end states checkable, feedback acquisition falls short in localization and lacks checkers for free-form goals, and loops open the agent edge into FA to buy either.
A value function or reward model refit on the agent’s trajectories follows the policy, letting F ν ( k ) F_{\nu}^{(k)} move with each round and inviting compounding reward hacking, exploited errors re-entering the data on which it is refit [ 14 , 135 , 144 ] .
MagicGUI-RMS slows this edge with a GPT-4o reward model that arbitrates its domain model [ 144 ] , yet both it and UI-Genie track their reward models only on fixed labeled sets, excluding the on-policy rollouts where exploitation would surface [ 135 ] .
A frozen self-judge keeps its bias fixed but shares it with the writer [ 162 , 278 , 279 , 281 , 201 ] , its errors cutting revision short, as the over-optimistic EvoSkill-GUI critic shows.
Verifiers fixed before the loop cut the edge, at the episode level in DART-GUI and MobileRL [ 115 , 17 ] and at the step level in SEAgent’s World State Model [ 16 ] , which localizes credit independently of the agent but exposes the loop to distribution shift once rollouts leave its training distribution.
Localization and free-form goal coverage thus cost either fidelity that follows the agent or, from a model fixed before the loop, distribution shift. VI-C UC Bound by Generalization Update consolidation in GUI and Web navigation persists the scored experience in two forms, weight updates and training-free writes, each method using one of them ( Table XI ).
Weight-updating methods write Φ θ \Phi_{\theta} with an update rule fixed before the loop, either continuously within a single training run [ 14 , 115 , 17 ] or over two or three rounds through GRPO with adversarial imitation, reinforcement learning on generated tasks, or fine-tuning of the agent and its reward model [ 16 , 15 , 144 , 135 ] , while SCALE fine-tunes its Selector and Predictor over repeated exploration rounds [ 284 ] .
Training-free methods keep the backbone frozen and let the acting model write narrative, episodic, procedural, or grounding memory [ 278 , 279 , 282 ] , tips and executable shortcuts [ 162 , 280 ] , or skill packages and executable APIs [ 281 , 201 ] , over one pass of a task stream, a few attempts per task, or a fixed budget of practice iterations, whereas a separate condenser writes the memory of WebCoach [ 163 ] . Retention and Generalization Constraints. Update consolidation is bound by generalization ( Table V ), documented within one platform and mostly on the task sets that produced the experience, whereas general capability, where measured, holds.
No method measures retention on earlier-round goals, and the closest evidence concerns general capability, ACuRL keeping or raising its scores on untrained environments by 2 to 7 points [ 15 ] .
Elsewhere, replay sustains iteration robustness [ 14 , 17 ] , and per-round scores rise without a reported reversal [ 135 , 144 , 284 ] , though non-monotonically in Mobile-Agent-E [ 162 ] , tracking 𝒥 k \mathcal{J}_{k} on the current evaluation set, not S ζ S_{\zeta} .
Training-free libraries evolve on their evaluation tasks [ 281 , 162 , 163 ] , as in several fixed loops [ 187 , 104 , 164 ] , measuring adaptation to a known goal distribution.
Held-out applications show transfer within the platform, MagicGUI-RMS gaining about as much on unseen apps as in domain [ 144 ] , whereas ACuRL reaches a new software version or operating system only by training there [ 15 ] .
Zero-shot transfer across platforms appears only in the fixed loop GUI-HARVEST, whose frozen harness, evolved on OSWorld, adds 6.47 points for Qwen3-VL-32B-Instruct and 13.87 for GPT-5 on WindowsAgentArena [ 286 ] .
Cross-form comparisons are confounded by backbone, training-free loops mostly running frozen proprietary models and weight-updating loops open ones. Author Checks and Reader Dependence. What a write carries into the agent is fixed in feedback acquisition, and the two forms of writing differ in what checks a write before it persists and, for non-parametric writes, in who reads it afterward.
Parametric writes pass on the verifier’s score alone, carrying its exposure unchanged, compounding hacking for agent-derived verifiers [ 14 , 135 , 144 , 284 ] and distribution shift for SEAgent’s frozen World State Model [ 16 ] , while exact checkers leave little to inherit [ 115 , 17 ] .
Non-parametric writes pass an admission test that mostly reuses the author’s judgment, EvoSkill-GUI and MobileSteward admitting skills and memories largely on their own critics’ verdicts [ 281 , 279 ] , whereas SkillWeaver unit-tests each synthesized API against execution feedback, a check outside the writer [ 201 ] .
Self-written skills can carry errors, such as the redundant or missing taps in the generated shortcuts of Mobile-Agent-E [ 162 ] , unlike the key- or validation-gated writes of multimodal reasoning.
Once admitted, a non-parametric write takes effect only when a later rollout reads it, its gain depending on the reader, as the memory that a separate condenser writes for WebCoach lowers Qwen2.5-VL-7B from 32.8% to 31.1% yet raises Skywork-r1v3-38B from 47.3% to 61.4% [ 163 ] .
A non-parametric write is thus checked mostly by its author and valued by its reader. VI-D Limits and Preconditions Bottleneck Attribution. In GUI and Web navigation, the quantity ceiling binds fixed and self-evolving loops alike, and self-reference leaves it in place, its cost lying in what each entry point puts at risk.
Fixed loops pay the same episode cost, scaling to thousands of virtual machines in ComputerRL [ 269 ] , and already train against signals fixed before the loop, the verification functions of ComputerRL at high fidelity and, below it, the screen judges of ZeroGUI [ 101 ] and MobileGUI-RL [ 130 ] .
A success-conditioned rule over a task set with fixed checkers risks little, only the allocation following the agent [ 17 ] , whereas generated goals move verification from fixed checkers to learned or prompted judges [ 16 , 15 , 284 , 201 ] , refit value functions and reward models buy localization with a fidelity that moves with the policy [ 14 , 135 , 144 ] , and a frozen backbone that judges and writes carries one bias into both roles [ 278 , 281 ] .
The higher return of each scarce episode recovers part of these costs, controlled gains being reported for reuse, reweighting, and goal generation.
No controlled comparison with fixed loops isolates these costs, which we read off the stage evidence and the provenance in Table XI . Cross-Cycle Degradation. Across cycles, self-reference opens paths along which degradation follows the agent, and the environment path adds a shift specific to the scenario.
On the goal side, success-conditioned rules and outcome-conditioned generators open the goal edge and agent-realized proposers extend the agent edge into σ ω \sigma_{\omega} [ 115 , 17 , 135 , 16 , 15 , 284 , 201 ] , inviting curriculum collapse, which MobileRL risks by design in removing persistently failing tasks [ 17 ] , and forgetting once the goal distribution moves, which replay mitigates [ 14 , 17 ] .
On the feedback side, refit verifiers extend the agent edge into FA, inviting compounding reward hacking untested beyond the two or three reported rounds, and training on self-generated trajectories invites model collapse [ 135 , 144 , 284 ] , whereas frozen self-judges share a fixed bias with the writer and open no such path.
The extension into UC carries erroneous writes, which Mobile-Agent-E reports within a single pass [ 162 ] , and the environment path carries the drift of live websites between cycles, independently of the agent [ 200 , 205 ] . Presupposed Resources. The mechanisms of GUI and Web navigation presuppose resources that its environments supply.
Repeated attempts at a goal, on which group sampling and reweighting rest, presuppose an environment that resets to a known state [ 115 , 17 ] , checkers and screen judges presuppose an end state that a program or a model can inspect [ 115 , 16 ] , and non-parametric reuse presupposes a reader able to exploit what the agent writes [ 163 ] . VII Self-Evolution in Embodied Manipulation Embodied manipulation occupies the quadrant of Figure 3 in which both ceilings are low on hardware, each rollout costing robot time, a reset, and wear, and each outcome checkable only by a per-task classifier or a learned judge.
Simulation relaxes both, where reinforcement learning against simulator success already forms fixed loops [ 62 , 287 , 288 ] and most self-evolving loops close before any hardware evaluation.
The methods differ chiefly in feedback source: simulator predicates keep fidelity, agent-derived verifiers densify feedback at the cost of an agent-tied bias, and external judges buy fidelity with per-query latency.
Self-reference enters mostly through these verifiers in weight-updating loops and through memory, skills, and code that a frozen model writes and judges in training-free loops.
Reported loops run at most tens of rounds or a bounded practice budget, and hardware evidence rests on eight to thirty evaluation trials per task.
